The following information was obtained by Liaison
supervisor I.V. Kuhrtz at the Intelligence Advisory Committee - Watch
Committee special meeting or September 21,
1957, relative to the
captioned matter.
The initial report in thismatter vas given by the
Preten one to ted te toes pended ap by radar
station at Montouk Point, Long Island, New York, at approrimately
4 p.m., proceeding in a westward direction with an altitude of
50,000 feet and a speed of 2,000 knots (approrimately 2300 miles per
The radar station at Montaut Point maintained an image for
approzimately one minute, and thereafter it was picked up by a radar
station at Benton, Pennsyluania (Wilkes-Barre, Pennsyluania), which
coincided with the wontouk Point pickup and vas tracked by Benton for
approtimately 9 minutes. Thereafter, jamming was reported by several
radar stations westwari as far as Chicago.
The ifatch Committee at its meeting on September 21 was told
by the dir Defense Command that the original reports had been down-
graded, since there was an Il-minute break in the tracking by the
various radai stations, and further that weather istations; in the
area were of the type which have in the past produced false radar
"pips" and electronic information. Also, there were a rumber of
Strategic dir Command planes in the Chicago area on a training flight.
The Watch Conmittee was told that the Air Defense Command has not
completed its investigation of this incident; however, in any event
it was reported that the phenomena reported west of Buffalo, New York,
were not related to the object as reported by Montauk Point ard
Benton, Pennsylvania.
The watch Committee concluded that, "It is highly improbable
that a Soviet operation is responsible for the unidentified flying
object reports of September, 1957."
Ihe watch conmittee in considering
this matter also concluded that there is no intelligence on Soviet
activities which can be related to a missile launching of this

UNIDENTIFIED FLYING OBJECT REPORTED
ON SEPTEMBER 20, 1957; INTELLIUELCE
ADVISOPY COMMITTEE - VATCH COMKITTEE
over the U.S. It is unlikely that a souiet aircraft could conduct
a mission at this
speed and
altitude and return to soviet territory.
The Soviets are given credit for the capability of a 'Submarine
launched cruise-type missile
Of low supersonic performance" and a
range of about 500 nautical miles; however, there is no evidence
of the eristence of such a missile by the soviets.
The Watch Committee also eramined possible Soviet motives
for launching o one way missile on an operation over the U.S., and
it was concluded that there would be little motivation at this tine
except possibly o psychological or retaliatory motive.
Committee ruled out one way reconnaissance operations
on the likelihood
that the results would be of small value and the rist of compromise
would be very great. (This possible retaliatory motiue by the Soviets
could be in
response to drsofiligade to a U.s.
"project Aquatone"
flight over the USSR which was detected on September 9,
